Sabha</a></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data" style="border-bottom:none"><span class="nowrap"><b>In office</b></span><br />1962<span style="display:none">&#160;(<span class="bday </td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background:lavender">Personal details</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data"><span style="display:none">(<span class="bday">1927-04-17</span>)</span>17 April 1927<br /><a href="/wiki/Ibrahimpatti" title="Ibrahimpatti">Ibrahimpatti</a>, <a href="/wiki/United_Provinces_of_British_India" class="mw-redirect" title="United Provinces of British India">United Provinces</a>, <a href="/wiki/British_India" class="mw-redirect" title="British India">British India</a> <br /> <span style="font-size:85%;">(present-day <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a>, India)</span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">8 July 2007<span style="display:none">(2007-07-08)</span> (aged&#160;80)<br /><a href="/wiki/New_Delhi" title="New Delhi">New Delhi</a>, <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Monuments</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Jannayak Sthal</a></td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Political party</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Samajwadi_Janata_Party_(Rashtriya)" title="Samajwadi Janata Party (Rashtriya)">Samajwadi class="infobox-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1126788409">.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol,.mw-parser-output .plainlist style="font-size:85%;">(1988 – 1990)</span></li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spouse</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Duja_Devi" class="mw-redirect" title="Duja Devi">Duja Devi</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Children</th><td class="infobox-data">2 sons (including <a href="/wiki/Neeraj_Shekhar" title="Neeraj Shekhar">Neeraj Shekhar</a>)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Alma_mater" title="Alma mater">Alma mater</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Allahabad_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad University">Allahabad University</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Signature</th><td class="infobox-data"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Chandrashekhar-Prime-Minister.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="Chandra Shekhar&#39;s signature"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Chandrashekhar-Prime-Minister.jpg/118px-Chandrashekhar-Prime-Minister.jpg" decoding="async" width="118" height="80" class="mw-file-element" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="border-top: 1px solid right; font-size: 95%"><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Chandra Shekhar</b> (17 April 1927 – 8 July 2007) was an Indian politician who served as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of India">8th</a> <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a>, between 10 November 1990 and 21 June 1991. He headed a minority government of a breakaway faction of the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a> with outside support from the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> He was the first Indian Prime Minister who had never held any prior government office.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His government was formed with the fewest party <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament" class="mw-redirect" title="Member of Parliament">MPs</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-IT_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IT-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> His government could not pass the budget<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> at a crucial time when <a href="/wiki/Moody%27s_Investors_Service" title="Moody&#39;s Investors Service">Moody</a> had downgraded India, and it further went down after the budget was not passed, and global credit-rating agencies further downgraded India from investment grade, making it impossible to even get short-term loans, and in no position to give any commitment to reform, the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a> and <a href="/wiki/IMF" class="mw-redirect" title="IMF">IMF</a> stopped their assistance. Shekhar had to authorise mortgaging of gold to avoid default of payment, and this action came in for particular criticism, as it was done secretly in the midst of the election.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CorbridgeHarriss2013_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CorbridgeHarriss2013-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_economic_crisis" title="1991 Indian economic crisis">1991 Indian economic crisis</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Assassination of Rajiv Gandhi">assassination of Rajiv Gandhi</a> plunged his government into crisis. But according to <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a>, finance minister <a href="/wiki/Yashwant_Sinha" title="Yashwant Sinha">Yashwant Sinha</a> mortgaged gold reserves without informing the Commerce Ministry (which was headed by Swamy).<sup id="cite_ref-:1_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> But granting the permission for US military planes to refuel in Indian airports during the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_War" title="Gulf War">Gulf War</a> improved the Prime Minister's image with the West.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_years_and_Education">Early years and Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early years and Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandra Shekhar was born on 17 April 1927 in a <a href="/wiki/Rajput" title="Rajput">Rajput</a> family at <a href="/wiki/Ibrahimpatti" title="Ibrahimpatti">Ibrahimpatti</a>, a village in Ballia, <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a>. He came from a farming family.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> He was awarded a Bachelor of Arts (graduate) degree at Satish Chandra P.G. College. He attended <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad University">Allahabad University</a>, obtaining his master's degree in political science in 1950.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> He was known as a firebrand in student politics and started his political career with Dr. <a href="/wiki/Ram_Manohar_Lohia" title="Ram Manohar Lohia">Ram Manohar Lohia</a>. After completing his graduation, he became active in socialist politics.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Family">Family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandra Shekhar married <a href="/wiki/Duja_Devi" class="mw-redirect" title="Duja Devi">Duja Devi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Duja Devi Degree College, a college founded in 1999 in <a href="/wiki/Ballia_district" title="Ballia district">Ballia district</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a> is named after her.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He had two sons with her, Pankaj Shekhar Singh and <a href="/wiki/Neeraj_Shekhar" title="Neeraj Shekhar">Neeraj Shekhar</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_life">Political life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Political life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Start_of_career">Start of career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Start of career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>He joined the socialist movement and was elected secretary of the district <a href="/wiki/Praja_Socialist_Party" title="Praja Socialist Party">Praja Socialist Party</a> (PSP), Ballia. Within a year, he was elected joint secretary of the PSP's State unit in Uttar Pradesh. In 1955–56, he took over as general secretary of the party in the State. His career as a parliamentarian began with his election to the Rajya Sabha from Uttar Pradesh in 1962. He came under the spell of <a href="/wiki/Acharya_Narendra_Dev" class="mw-redirect" title="Acharya Narendra Dev">Acharya Narendra Dev</a>, a fiery Socialist leader at the beginning of his political career. From 1962 to 1977, Shekhar was a member of <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>, the Upper house of the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_India" title="Parliament of India">Parliament of India</a>. He was elected to <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> from Uttar Pradesh on 3 April 1962 as an independent candidate and completed his tenure on 2 April 1968. After this, he was re-elected twice to <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> from Uttar Pradesh as an INC candidate from 3 April 1968 to 2 April 1974 and from 3 April 1974 to 2 April 1980. He resigned from <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> on 2 March 1977 after he had been elected to <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a> from <a href="/wiki/Ballia" title="Ballia">Ballia</a>. When the <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">emergency</a> was declared, even though he was a Congress party politician, he was arrested and sent to <a href="/wiki/Patiala" title="Patiala">Patiala</a> jail.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Join_Congress">Join Congress</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Join Congress">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandra Shekhar was a prominent leader of the socialists. He joined <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress</a> in 1964. From 1962 to 1967, he was a member of the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>. He first entered the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a> in 1977. He came to be known as a 'young Turk' for his conviction and courage in the fight against the vested interests. The other 'young Turks', who formed the 'ginger group' in the Congress in the fight for egalitarian policies, included<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> leaders like <a href="/wiki/Feroze_Gandhi" title="Feroze Gandhi">Feroze Gandhi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Satyendra_Narayan_Sinha" title="Satyendra Narayan Sinha">Satyendra Narayan Sinha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mohan_Dharia" title="Mohan Dharia">Mohan Dharia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ram_Dhan" title="Ram Dhan">Ram Dhan</a>. As a member of the Congress Party, he vehemently criticised Indira Gandhi for her declaration of emergency in 1975. Chandrashekhar was arrested during the emergency and sent to prison along with other "young turks".<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Bharat_Yatra_.281983.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Bharat_Yatra_(1983)">Bharat Yatra (1983)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Bharat Yatra (1983)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandra Shekhar went on a nationwide <i><a href="/wiki/Padayatra" title="Padayatra">padayatra</a></i> in 1983 from Kanyakumari to New Delhi,<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> to know the country better, which he claimed gave jitters to Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> He was called a "Young Turk".<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> He travelled nearly 4,260&#160;km and nearly six months.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Chandra Shekhar started his Bharat Yatra from <a href="/wiki/Kanyakumari" title="Kanyakumari">Kanyakumari</a> on 6, the same day that his party, <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a> was swept to power in <a href="/wiki/1983_Karnataka_Legislative_Assembly_election" title="1983 Karnataka Legislative Assembly election">Karnataka</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> He finished his march at <a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Rajghat</a> in New Delhi on 25 June, the eighth anniversary of the declaration of the <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">Emergency</a> and also the day India won the <a href="/wiki/1983_Cricket_World_Cup_Final" class="mw-redirect" title="1983 Cricket World Cup Final">Cricket World Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Chandra Shekhar established Bharat Yatra Centres in various parts of the country and set up a Bharat Yatra Trust in <a href="/wiki/Gurugram_Airstrip" title="Gurugram Airstrip">Bhondsi village</a> in Haryana's Gurgaon to focus on rural development.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>  "Bharat Yatra Kendra" "Bhondsi ashram" was set up by the Chandra Shekhar in 1983 on 600 acre of <a href="/wiki/Panchayati_raj_in_India" title="Panchayati raj in India">panchayat</a> land,<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> where godman <a href="/wiki/Chandraswami" title="Chandraswami">Chandraswami</a> and godman's associate <a href="/wiki/Adnan_Khashoggi" title="Adnan Khashoggi">Adnan Khashoggi</a> (a Saudi Arabian billionaire international arms dealer embroiled in various scandals) use to visit him.<sup id="cite_ref-bhond7_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bhond7-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-bhond8_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bhond8-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-bhond11_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bhond11-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Before 2002, some of the government land of the ashram was taken back by the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Haryana" title="Government of Haryana">Government of Haryana</a> on the instructions of then Chief Minister <a href="/wiki/Om_Prakash_Chautala" title="Om Prakash Chautala">Om Prakash Chautala</a> (in office 1989-91 and 1999-2004).<sup id="cite_ref-bhond9_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bhond9-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> In 2002, <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_India" title="Supreme Court of India">Supreme Court of India</a> returned most of the land, barring some land, to the Bhondsi <a href="/wiki/Gram_panchayat" title="Gram panchayat">gram panchayat</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bhond10_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bhond10-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="In_Janata_Party">In Janata Party</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: In Janata Party">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandrasekhar was jailed during the emergency and after, he became the President of <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>. In the parliamentary elections, <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>  formed the government after the <a href="/wiki/1977_Indian_general_election" title="1977 Indian general election">1977 Indian general election</a> headed by <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a>. However the party lost the 1980 elections and were routed in <a href="/wiki/1984_Indian_general_election" title="1984 Indian general election">1984 Indian general election</a> winning just 10 seats and Chandrasekhar losing his own <a href="/wiki/Ballia_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ballia (Lok Sabha constituency)">Ballia seat</a> to <a href="/wiki/Jagannath_Chowdhary" title="Jagannath Chowdhary">Jagannath Chowdhary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-loksabha1984_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-loksabha1984-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In May 1988, he resigned from <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>'s President post when <a href="/wiki/Lokdal" title="Lokdal">Lok Dal</a> (A) was merged with <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>. <a href="/wiki/Ajit_Singh_(politician)" title="Ajit Singh (politician)">Ajit Singh</a> was made president of <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/George_Fernandes" title="George Fernandes">George Fernandes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Biju_Patnaik" title="Biju Patnaik">Biju Patnaik</a>, <a href="/wiki/Madhu_Dandavate" title="Madhu Dandavate">Madhu Dandavate</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ramakrishna_Hegde" title="Ramakrishna Hegde">Ramakrishna Hegde</a> opposed this merger with <a href="/wiki/Lokdal" title="Lokdal">Lok Dal</a> (A) but <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yashwant_Sinha" title="Yashwant Sinha">Yashwant Sinha</a> and <a href="/wiki/Suryadeo_Singh" title="Suryadeo Singh">Suryadeo Singh</a> supported this move.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1988, his party merged with other parties and formed the government under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/V.P._Singh" class="mw-redirect" title="V.P. Singh">V.P. Singh</a>. Again his relationship with the coalition deteriorated and he formed another party, <a href="/wiki/Samajwadi_Janata_Party_(Rashtriya)" title="Samajwadi Janata Party (Rashtriya)">Janata Dal (Socialist)</a> faction. With the support of Congress (I) headed by <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a>, he replaced V.P. Singh as the Prime Minister of India in November 1990. After 1977, he was elected to Lok Sabha in all the elections, except in 1984 when the Congress swept the polls after Indira Gandhi's assassination. The post of Prime Minister, which he thought he genuinely deserved, eluded him in 1989 when V. P. Singh pipped him at the post and was chosen to head the first coalition government at the centre.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Deposing_V._P._Singh">Deposing V. P. Singh</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Deposing V. P. Singh">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandra Shekhar seized the moment and left the Janata Dal with several of his own supporters to form the <a href="/wiki/Samajwadi_Janata_Party_(Rashtriya)" title="Samajwadi Janata Party (Rashtriya)">Samajwadi Janata Party/Janata Dal (Socialist)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> He won a confidence motion with the support of his 64 MPs and <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a>, the leader of the Opposition, and was sworn in as Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Eight Janata Dal MPs who voted for this motion were disqualified by the speaker <a href="/wiki/Rabi_Ray" title="Rabi Ray">Rabi Ray</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="In_Parliament">In Parliament</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: In Parliament">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandra Shekhar was a member of Rajya Sabha from 1962 to 1977, 1962 to 1968 as an independent supported by Socialist Party and later as member of Congress. He was jailed during the Emergency. After his release from jail in 1977, he joined Janata Party. He was elected to Lok Sabha from <a href="/wiki/Ballia_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ballia (Lok Sabha constituency)">Ballia</a> as a member of various incarnations of Janata Party in 1977, 1980, 1989, 1991, 1996, 1998, 1999, and 2004. He lost that seat only once in that span, in 1984 election. After his death, his son Neeraj Shekhar won the ensuing by-poll in 2008.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_Ministries">Other Ministries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Other Ministries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span id="Minister_of_Information_and_Broadcasting_.281990-1991.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Minister_of_Information_and_Broadcasting_(1990-1991)">Minister of Information and Broadcasting (1990-1991)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Minister of Information and Broadcasting (1990-1991)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Chandra Shekhar remained the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_and_Broadcasting_(India)" title="Ministry of Information and Broadcasting (India)">Minister of Information and Broadcasting</a> from 21 November 1990 to 21 June 1991 from <a href="/wiki/Samajwadi_Janata_Party_(Rashtriya)" title="Samajwadi Janata Party (Rashtriya)">Samajwadi Janata Party (Rashtriya)</a> and at that time he was himself the Prime Minister of India.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> He was preceded by <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V. P. Singh</a> and succeeded by <a href="/wiki/P._V._Narasimha_Rao" title="P. V. Narasimha Rao">P. V. Narasimha Rao</a> to the position after he resigned from the position of Prime Minister due to loss of support of the alliances.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Minister_of_Home_Affairs_.281990-1991.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Minister_of_Home_Affairs_(1990-1991)">Minister of Home Affairs (1990-1991)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Minister of Home Affairs (1990-1991)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Like the Minister of I and B, he remained <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of Home Affairs (India)">Minister of Home Affairs</a> for the time period of 7 months. He was himself the Prime Minister at that time and was preceded by <a href="/wiki/Mufti_Mohammad_Sayeed" title="Mufti Mohammad Sayeed">Mufti Mohammad Sayeed</a> and succeeded by <a href="/wiki/Shankarrao_Chavan" title="Shankarrao Chavan">Shankarrao Chavan</a> of <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Minister_of_Defence_.281990-1991.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Minister_of_Defence_(1990-1991)">Minister of Defence (1990-1991)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Minister of Defence (1990-1991)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Along with <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Ministry of Home Affairs (India)">Ministry of Home Affairs</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_and_Broadcasting_(India)" title="Ministry of Information and Broadcasting (India)">Information and Broadcasting</a>, he also handled <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(India)" title="Ministry of Defence (India)">Ministry of Defence</a> under him as the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a>. He was Minister of Defence for a very short time of 7 months and didn't present the Defence budget.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> He was preceded by <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V. P. Singh</a> and succeeded by <a href="/wiki/P._V._Narasimha_Rao" title="P. V. Narasimha Rao">P. V. Narasimha Rao</a> as the Minister of Defence.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Swearing-in_ceremony_of_Shri_Chandra_Sekhar_as_the_Prime_Minister_of_India_at_Rashtrapati_Bhavan_forecourt.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Swearing-in_ceremony_of_Shri_Chandra_Sekhar_as_the_Prime_Minister_of_India_at_Rashtrapati_Bhavan_forecourt.jpg/220px-Swearing-in_ceremony_of_Shri_Chandra_Sekhar_as_the_Prime_Minister_of_India_at_Rashtrapati_Bhavan_forecourt.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Swearing-in_ceremony_of_Shri_Chandra_Sekhar_as_the_Prime_Minister_of_India_at_Rashtrapati_Bhavan_forecourt.jpg/330px-Swearing-in_ceremony_of_Shri_Chandra_Sekhar_as_the_Prime_Minister_of_India_at_Rashtrapati_Bhavan_forecourt.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Swearing-in_ceremony_of_Shri_Chandra_Sekhar_as_the_Prime_Minister_of_India_at_Rashtrapati_Bhavan_forecourt.jpg/440px-Swearing-in_ceremony_of_Shri_Chandra_Sekhar_as_the_Prime_Minister_of_India_at_Rashtrapati_Bhavan_forecourt.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2500" data-file-height="1752" /></a><figcaption>Swearing-in ceremony of Shri Chandra Sekhar as the Prime Minister of India at Rashtrapati Bhavan forecourt</figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister">Prime Minister</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Prime Minister">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Chandra_Shekhar_ministry" title="Chandra Shekhar ministry">Chandra Shekhar ministry</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mohammad_Mosaddak_Ali_met_with_Prime_Minister_of_India_Chandra_Shekhar_Singh_at_Zia_International_Airport_in_Dhaka.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Mohammad_Mosaddak_Ali_met_with_Prime_Minister_of_India_Chandra_Shekhar_Singh_at_Zia_International_Airport_in_Dhaka.jpg/220px-Mohammad_Mosaddak_Ali_met_with_Prime_Minister_of_India_Chandra_Shekhar_Singh_at_Zia_International_Airport_in_Dhaka.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Mohammad_Mosaddak_Ali_met_with_Prime_Minister_of_India_Chandra_Shekhar_Singh_at_Zia_International_Airport_in_Dhaka.jpg/330px-Mohammad_Mosaddak_Ali_met_with_Prime_Minister_of_India_Chandra_Shekhar_Singh_at_Zia_International_Airport_in_Dhaka.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Mohammad_Mosaddak_Ali_met_with_Prime_Minister_of_India_Chandra_Shekhar_Singh_at_Zia_International_Airport_in_Dhaka.jpg/440px-Mohammad_Mosaddak_Ali_met_with_Prime_Minister_of_India_Chandra_Shekhar_Singh_at_Zia_International_Airport_in_Dhaka.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1417" data-file-height="979" /></a><figcaption>Mohammad Mosaddak Ali met with Prime Minister of India Chandra Shekhar Singh at Zia International Airport in Dhaka</figcaption></figure>
<p>Chandra Shekhar was prime minister for seven months, the second shortest period after that of <a href="/wiki/Charan_Singh" title="Charan Singh">Charan Singh</a>. <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a> was instrumental in forming this government with the support of Congress.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>  He also handled the portfolios of Defence and Home Affairs during this period. However, his government could not introduce a full budget because on 6 March 1991 Congress withdrew support during its formulation.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> As a result, Chandra Shekhar resigned the office of the prime minister after 15 days on 21 June.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> was his Economic Advisor.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a> along with <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> and <a href="/wiki/Montek_Singh_Ahluwalia" title="Montek Singh Ahluwalia">Montek Singh Ahluwalia</a> prepared a series of documents on economic liberalization but could not pass in parliament because Congress withdrew support.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Jairam_Ramesh" title="Jairam Ramesh">Jairam Ramesh</a> in his book <i>To the Brink and Back: India’s 1991 Story</i> has written that "Chandrashekhar's Cabinet Committee on Trade and Investment (CCTI) itself had on 11 March 1991 approved the new export strategy which contained the main elements of the 4 July package".<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-premiership">Post-premiership</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Post-premiership">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After handing the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime Minister">premiership</a> to <a href="/wiki/P._V._Narasimha_Rao" title="P. V. Narasimha Rao">P. V. Narasimha Rao</a>, Chandra Shekar's political importance was reduced, although he was able to retain his seat in the Lok Sabha for many years afterward.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Priyaranjan_Dasmunsi_laying_wreath_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre,_in_Delhi_on_July_09,_2007.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Priyaranjan_Dasmunsi_laying_wreath_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg/220px-Priyaranjan_Dasmunsi_laying_wreath_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Priyaranjan_Dasmunsi_laying_wreath_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg/330px-Priyaranjan_Dasmunsi_laying_wreath_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Priyaranjan_Dasmunsi_laying_wreath_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg/440px-Priyaranjan_Dasmunsi_laying_wreath_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1463" /></a><figcaption>Priyaranjan Dasmunsi laying wreath at the mortal remains of the former Prime Minister, Shri Chandra Shekhar at the funeral pyre, in Delhi on 9 July 2007</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister,_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar,_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08,_2007_(1).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08%2C_2007_%281%29.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08%2C_2007_%281%29.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08%2C_2007_%281%29.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1528" /></a><figcaption>Prime Minister Dr. Manmohan Singh paying tribute to the mortal remains of the former Prime Minister Shri Chandra Shekhar, in New Delhi on 8 July 2007</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister,_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_homage_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre,_in_Delhi_on_July_09,_2007.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_homage_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_homage_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_homage_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_at_the_funeral_pyre%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3008" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption>The Prime Minister, Dr. Manmohan Singh paying homage to the mortal remains of the former Prime Minister, Shri Chandra Shekhar at the funeral pyre, in Delhi on 9 July 2007</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar,_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08,_2007.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/The_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08%2C_2007.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="156" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/The_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08%2C_2007.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/The_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_paying_tribute_to_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_08%2C_2007.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1562" /></a><figcaption>The former Prime Minister Shri Atal Bihari Vajpayee paying tribute to the mortal remains of the former Prime Minister Shri Chandra Shekhar, in New Delhi on 8 July 2007</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_carriage_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_making_its_way_to_Ekta_Sthal_for_the_state_funeral,_in_Delhi_on_July_09,_2007_(1).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/The_carriage_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_making_its_way_to_Ekta_Sthal_for_the_state_funeral%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007_%281%29.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="246" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/The_carriage_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_making_its_way_to_Ekta_Sthal_for_the_state_funeral%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007_%281%29.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/The_carriage_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Chandra_Shekhar_making_its_way_to_Ekta_Sthal_for_the_state_funeral%2C_in_Delhi_on_July_09%2C_2007_%281%29.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1877" data-file-height="2100" /></a><figcaption>The carriage carrying the mortal remains of the former Prime Minister, Shri Chandra Shekhar making its way to Ekta Sthal for the state funeral, in Delhi on 9 July 2007</figcaption></figure>
<p>Chandra Shekhar died on 8 July 2007. He had been suffering with <a href="/wiki/Multiple_myeloma" title="Multiple myeloma">multiple myeloma</a> for some time and had been in the <a href="/wiki/Apollo_Hospital,_Indraprastha" title="Apollo Hospital, Indraprastha">Apollo Hospital</a> at New Delhi since May. He was survived by two sons.<sup id="cite_ref-hindu20070709_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindu20070709-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Politicians from across the spectrum of Indian parties paid tribute to him<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> and the government of India declared seven days of state mourning.<sup id="cite_ref-hindu20070709_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindu20070709-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> He was cremated with full state honours on a traditional <a href="/wiki/Funeral_pyre" class="mw-redirect" title="Funeral pyre">funeral pyre</a> at <b>Jannayak Sthal</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> on the banks of the river <a href="/wiki/Yamuna" title="Yamuna">Yamuna</a>, on 10 July.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> In August, his ashes were immersed in the river <a href="/wiki/Siruvani" class="mw-redirect" title="Siruvani">Siruvani</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Shekhar&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li id="cite_note-14"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-14">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The rise of Socialist politics under Chandra Shekhar. <i>In the Janata-coalition government that came to power in the aftermath of that election, Chandra Shekhar willingly gave up his claim to a Cabinet ministerial role that he was offered in favour of his fellow-Young Turk Mohan Dharia. That was just one instance of Chandra Shekhar’s deep commitment and loyalty towards his friends which is a recurring theme in the book. A rare occurrence in an opportunistic political world—Chandra Shekhar was also upright and forthright, not prone to hypocrisy like the commonplace politicians. Sometimes his straight talk stunned even seasoned politicians like Ram Manohar Lohia and Indira Gandhi.</i></span>
</li>
